|PROGRAM|Establishment of the Santa Rosa City College|
|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|Santa Rosa City College Technical Working Group composed of CPDO, CE, SP, MO, City Admin, CB, CA, PUP Santa Rosa, and DepEd Santa Rosa.<br><br>|
|Brief Description| |
|This program involves the conversion “Dangal ng Pagbabago” Rehabilitation Facility and part of the City Housing Facility into a Tertiary Educational Facility. The total target area would be 4,260 square meters.| |
|Program Justification| |
|The City of Santa Rosa also has a scarcity with college institutions. Six (6) were private institutions, while PUP Santa Rosa Laguna Campus (PUP-SRLC) is the only public college institution subsidized by the City Government. PUP-SRLC can only accommodate up to 800 freshmen students every year, while SHS Graduates in Santa Rosa is already at 1,800 annually. Also, business locators in Santa Rosa are already at 120 and in desperate need of manpower resource. There is a high demand for professional graduates of engineering, business, administration, management, information technology, and even technical-vocational courses.| |
|Intended Beneficiaries| |
|- Graduating Senior High School (SHS) residing at the City of Santa Rosa<br>- Other graduating SHS Students<br>| |

|Components|Estimated Cost|
|---|---|
|Phases:<br><br>1. Feasibility Study<br>2. Identification of courses to be offered<br><br> Courses not offered at PUP Santa Rosa<br> Courses/Skills needed by locators<br> Projected in-demand courses (in 5 to 10 years from now)<br> Niche positioning (Technical, I.T., Business, Medical, etc.)<br><br><br>3. Financial Plan<br>4. TOR and Bidding<br>5. Construction<br>6. Organizational Plan<br>7. CHED Accreditation<br>8. Start of Operation<br><br><br>|Construction of 2-storey building with roof deck at Php 500,000,000.00|
| |Timetable|
| |Phases:<br><br>1. Feasibility Study (4 months – done)<br>2. Course identification (6 months)<br>3. Financial Plan (3 months)<br>4. TOR and Bidding (3 months)<br>5. Construction (4 years)<br>|
|Target Output/Success Indicators|Possible Risks|
|1. Reduced number of “Out-of school-Youth”<br>2. Increased number of Graduate Professionals<br>3. Increased manpower and professional resource<br>4. Increased income generation from taxes of graduate professionals<br>|1. Lack of funds<br>2. Lack of political will<br>3. Short-exchange in terms of plan execution<br>4. Availability of workforce in operating the institution<br>|
|Expected Private Sector Response| |
|Their participation is highly expected, especially from the business sector because they will also benefit from this program by having a secured pool of graduate professionals that will suffice to their needs for manpower resources.| |

|PROGRAM|College Educational Assistance Program (CEAP) and the creation of City Scholarship Database Management System for CEAP Grantees<br><br>|
|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|City Government of Santa Rosa<br><br>• City Scholarship Office<br><br>• City Budget Office, Accounting Office, Treasury Office<br><br>• City Information and Technology Office<br><br>• Third-party Creator<br>|
|Brief Description| |
|One of the priorities of the City Government of Santa Rosa, Laguna, is to support education of deserving students of the city. It is for this reason that the Educational Assistance Program for College students had been conceptualized.<br><br>The program aims to give financial assistance to the deserving college students with extreme financial need. Every beneficiary will be given a maximum amount of Php5,000.00 every semester as additional assistance for their matriculation and other educational requirements.<br><br>The program has approximately 3,600 scholars for the year 2023 and will continue to grow every year.<br><br>And with the increasing number of recipients, there’s a growing need to have a database management system that will house all the important information of our CEAP Scholars. The online database will help make the application and evaluation process better, as everything will be in one place.<br><br>It would also make the information updates, i.e. contact numbers, application status, much easier.| |
|Program Justification| |
|The City Scholarship Office of the City Government of Santa Rosa creates programs that aim to provide educational assistance College students through CEAP (College Educational Assistance Program) to give deserving students an opportunity to continue their studies and build their careers for a successful future. College educational assistance can cover and helps student with school expenses. They can use the money to pay for tuition, books and other school related expenses.<br><br>The program has approximately 3,600 scholars for the year 2023 and will continue to grow every year.<br><br>With the increasing number of recipients, there’s a growing need to have a database management system that will house all the important information of our CEAP Scholars. One of the goals of the program is to make the application process purely online where all the requirements will be uploaded through the system. This would lessen the application phase significantly. It would also make the evaluation process easier, as everything is in one place.<br><br>Each scholar will have an account that would give access to their profile, so that they can easily update their information. It would also show their application and scholarship status.<br><br>This would also address the problem on physical storage. And by going paperless, we know that it would benefit the environment.| |

|Intended Beneficiaries| |
|---|---|
|The program will benefit the City Scholarship Office and the recipients of CEAP (College Educational Assistance Program)| |
|Components|Estimated Cost|
|CEAP (College Educational Assistance Program)<br><br>• Application<br>• Interview<br>• Screening<br><br><br>CEAP Data Management System<br><br>• Data encoding<br>• App Creation and Maintenance<br>• Procurement of equipment for the Database System<br>• Tech Requirements<br><br><br>o Internet Access<br>o Database storage<br>|CEAP (College Educational Assistance Program) : Php54,000,000.00 (5400 Recipients)<br><br>CEAP Database Management System : Php1,000,000.00 for the development and maintenance|
| |Timetable|
| |CEAP (College Educational Assistance Program) : four (4) to five (5) years<br><br>CEAP Database Management System: Five (5) years (with maintenance and upgrade)|
|Target Output/Success Indicators|Possible Risks|

|PROGRAM|Free Anti-Rabies Mass Vaccination| |
|---|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|● CVO<br><br>● CHO 1& 2 (City Health Office)<br><br>● SP (Sangguniang Panlungsod)<br><br>● CIO (City Information Office)<br><br>● ABC (Association of Barangay Chairman)<br><br>● HOA( Homeowner’s assc. Officers)<br>| |
|Brief Description| | |
|In 2022, the Philippines is still ranked 6th in the World with the highest incidence of Rabies cases,with that, the National Government’s target of totally eliminating RABIES in the Philippines by 2030. The City Government of Santa Rosa through the City Veterinary Office is gunning to vaccinate 70% of the Pet population in the City, along with other strategies the CVO is implementing under its OPLAN: ALIS PROGRAM under the National Rabies Control Program of the Department of Agriculture-Bureau of Animal Industry.| | |
|Program Justification| | |
|With the City’s increasing population that goes hand in hand with the increased number of pets that they bring along with them,thus justifying the rise in Animal Bite Cases and the need for additional Anti-Rabies vaccines in the City. In order to somehow counteract the possible human & pet Rabies outbreak.| | |
|Intended Beneficiaries| | |
|Pet owners of the City of Santa Rosa| | |
|Components| |Estimated Cost|
|1. Additional purchase of vaccine to cover for 44,409 pets which is roughly 10% of the City’s population.<br>2. Vaccinate pets in all 200 subdivisions/villages in the City. Currently the CVO vaccinates 164 out of 200 since other areas refuse the Program, In that regard;<br>| |Php. 2.470,000 for 44,409 pets|

|3. Meeting with the ABC to help the CVO convince the other Subdivisions for compulsory pet vaccination.<br>4. More Informative posting on Rabies and Rabies-related activities on the City Government of Santa Rosa Facebook Page, in coordination with the CIO.<br>5. Quarterly IEC with the City Health Offices regarding Rabies prevention , Eradication & control strategies with the Barangay.<br>6. Amendment on the City of Santa Rosa Anti-Rabies Ordinance ( City Ord.1590-2008)<br><br><br>|Timetable|
|---|---|
| |2022 to 2028|
|Target Output/Success Indicators|Possible Risks|
|1. Number of Pets Vaccinated (Increased)<br>2. Number of Household served (Increased)<br>3. No of Rabies cases (Decreased)<br>4. No of Rabies-related deaths (Zero-case)<br>|1. Insufficient funding<br>2. Change in Policies brought about by change in administration<br>|
|Expected Private Sector Response| |
|Since the project is free, positive responses will be gained.| |

|---|---|---|

|The CVO will be vaccinating twice a year the New Castle Disease (NCD) –Lasota Strain a very virulent –type of poultry disease that causes massive financial losses for poultry industry. Intended to support poultry raisers (Enlisted in the CVO Ani at Kita Profile & new ones) of Game fowls, Heritage, Native free ranged chickens.| | |

With the CVO providing them with technical support like vaccination that prevents disease outbreaks thus lessen losses, hence sustainable financial merit is achieved.| | |

| | |Continous –Annually|

|PROGRAM|Mandatory Pet Microchipping Program| |
|---|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|City Veterinary Office (CVO), City Information Office (CIO), Barangay and Homeowners Association Officers.<br><br>| |
|Brief Description| | |
|Microchips are permanent forms of identification that are difficult to alter. One time, lifetime identification, That justifies the legal bonds of owner and pet. Aids the City Veterinary Office in identifying stray animals, non-abiding pet owners and for pet registration, Wherein pet owners can freely check their pet’s record since there is a downloadable APP and of course it is paperless.| | |
|Program Justification| | |
|Pet owners in the City of Santa Rosa. By virtue of an SP resolution that a portion of the price will be shouldered by the owner in order to continue the program.free for the indigents. But for owners of impounded pets, the entire amount of the microchip will be added to the penalty which is 500 + Microchip cost for the first Offense, 1000 + Microchip cost 2nd offense plus 8 hour Community Service in the Barangay Hall where they reside and 1500+microchip cost plus 24 hours community service at the Santa Rosa City Slaughterhouse for the 3rd offense, 4th offense will disallow the violator to have their pets (confiscation). Microchipping should be performed together with Anti-Rabies Vaccination.| | |
|Intended Beneficiaries| | |
|All Pet owners(dogs & cats) regardless of the number and breed of pet in their possession. Nowadays Microchipping is a must for breeding animals and animals intended for accompanying its owners in both Domestic and international travel.| | |
|Components| |Estimated Cost|
|1. Additional annual Purchase of Petdenty Microchip included in the AIP.<br>2. Program announcement on the City Government Of Santa Rosa Facebook Page<br>| |Php. 250 to 350 per microchip, pets 10-20% of the human population of the City (42,000) 300 x 42000= Php 7,500,000 until 2028|

|3. Introduction via lectures to end users regarding the mandatory microchipping of pets.<br>4. Scheduling of Walk-in and Mass Microchipping per Barangay.<br>5. Program implementation (continuous)<br><br><br>|Timetable|
|---|---|
| |2022 to 2028|
|Target Output/Success Indicators|Possible Risks|
|1. Increased in the number of registered pets.<br>2. Lessen the number of unwanted and roaming pets<br>3. Lessen Animal Rabies cases, since the pet owner can freely check the status of their Rabies vaccination of their pet(s) on their mobile gadgets due to the downloadable APP.<br>|1. Insufficient funding<br>2. The Development of a more improved, perhaps more technologically advanced Pet Identification-registration method.<br>3. Change in administration, wherein this project is not in the priority list.<br>|
|Expected Private Sector Response| |
|Since this program is not entirely free, mixed response is expected.| |

|---|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|TWG;<br><br>● LCE<br><br>● CPDO<br><br>● City Legal<br><br>● CVO<br><br>● CENRO<br><br>● City Treasury Office<br><br>● BPLO<br><br>● City Engineering Office<br>| |
|Brief Description| | |
|The Santa Rosa City Slaughterhouse in 2009 entered into a Public-Private Partnership (even before the PPP law was enacted) by means of a 20 year lease contract. |PROGRAM|Infectious/ Pathological Waste Treatment Facility (Pyrolysis)<br><br>| |
|---|---|---|
|PROPONENTS|City Environment and Natural Resources Office- City Government of Santa Rosa Laguna<br><br>| |
|Brief Description| | |
|Infectious and pathological waste is also known as medical or bio hazardous waste. This refers to any waste material generated during medical procedures or in healthcare facilities that has a potential to cause diseases or pose a risk to human health. This type of waste requires special handling, treatment, and disposal to prevent the spread of any infectious and protect the environment. Relative to that, the process/ treatment that involved to the said type of waste is called Pyrolysis. It is a chemical process involves degradation of organic materials in the total absence of oxygen or with limited oxygen supply. It is a form of thermal degradation that occurs at high temperatures, typically between 400 to 1200 degrees Celsius.| | |
|Program Justification| | |
|1. The escalating huge amount of 414.812 metric tons of waste per day and the challenges of solid waste management disposal and segregation.<br>2. High cost and expenses of outsourced private entity for the use of Sanitary Land Fill as waste disposal facility.<br>| | |
|Intended Beneficiaries| | |
|1. The community of Santa Rosa will be directly the beneficiaries of the project.<br>2. The public and private health care facilities will also a beneficiary since their waste will be collected and processed in the facility which can eventually result to a business enterprise.<br>| | |
|Components| |Estimated Cost|
|1. Scheduling for the collection of waste.<br>2. Schematic process of the collected waste using the pyrolysis technology.<br>3. Generation of data from the collected and processed waste<br>4. Allocation of intended price per kilo/s as a basis for the payment of the private sectors.<br>5. Compliance to the EMB for the standard of the facility.<br><br><br>| |Php 368 Million|
| | |Timetable|
| | |2022 to 2024 (by virtue of Executive Order 138, s.2021)|

|Target Output/Success Indicators|Possible Risks|
|---|---|
|1. This project will minimize the expenses of waste collection and disposal by other suppliers by 50% for the first year.<br>2. Facility can generate the highest possible revenues in the market by turning its waste into various forms that soon be sold in the market.<br>3. The City may have a chance to achieve its target and that is to have Zero Waste and reduce pollution by the extraction and processing of its own waste.<br>4. Improving the City’s disposal, segregation and waste management by utilizing the potential of the facility.<br>5. Avoidance of land, air, and water pollution as to benefits social health.<br>|1. Private waste processing facilities similar to the Infectious/ Pathological Waste Treatment Facility (Pyrolysis) would be installed with the period.<br>2. Proportional to the increase of population, there is an increase of generated waste also.<br>|
|Expected Private Sector Response| |
|May pose negative responses due to the “Not in my backyard” phenomenon.| |

#### 4.3. RESOURCE MOBILIZATION STRATEGIES

###### INCOME SOURCES

The City of Santa Rosa has the following financing sources:

- 1. Internal Sources. These are local sources consisting of tax and non-tax revenues. Tax revenues include local taxes from business, community tax, real property, other local taxes, fines and penalties, professional tax, amusement tax, franchise tax, printing and publication tax, property tax, and tax on delivery trucks and van. Non-tax revenues are incomes from permits and licenses, service income, business income and other income covering dividend income, interest income and miscellaneous income.
- 2. External Sources. These are the national tax allotment (NTA)/ internal revenue allotment (IRA), transfers from National Government Agencies which are budgetary transfers and/or grants from the national government bodies, donations from national government agencies and private sectors, shares from economic zones, PAGCOR/PCSO and other institutions and partnerships from other LGUs and development partners.
- 3. Other Sources of Funds. In cases where regular sources are insufficient, some projects are funded through loans from banks; international financing institutions; and through public-private partnerships and the like.

The total real property tax revenue of the city in FY 2022 is amounting to PhP 1.230 Billion. Increasing trend in the collection from CY 2019 to CY 2022 was shown in Table 4.1. In CY 2020, Sangguniang Panlungsod Resolution issued a real property tax (RPT) amnesty. This, plus the digitization of services are the strategies that the city implemented during the time of pandemic to collect Real Property Taxes.

The total local revenue sources are decreasing from 2019 to 2021 with a huge percentage increase in 2022 as seen in Table 4.3. Extra-ordinary collection from CY 2018 was realized that resulted to the huge collection for the said year. Large part of the collection came from Business Fees and Licenses. The highest percentage increase of 23.54% increase in local revenue was the result of various economic recovery strategies made by the city.

###### EXPENDITURES

Majority of the city’s expenditures were allocated to General Public Services for the operation of 48 offices as seen in Table 4.5 and the highest amount spent in this service was in CY 2022. On the other hand, Social Services, Economic Services and Other Services incurred a huge increase in 2021 which was similar to the obligated debt expenditure of the city in that same year as shown in Table 4.5. The city secured loans from Land bank’s RISE-UP LGUs Program to finance its recovery projects in CY 2021 and 2022.

###### COLLECTION EFFICIENCY

Collection prior to the COVID-19 pandemic has been good with an average growth rate of approximately 21% from 2015 to 2018. During the pandemic, the city was financially challenged as manifested in table 4.3 where the local revenue collection is decreasing in trend since onsite transactions are limited until contactless transactions are adapted in the early part of 2021. The city’s collection became more efficient as it embraced the new normal through adaption of eregistration and e-payments on its transactions and provision of tax amnesty to its clients and residents.

###### SELF-RELIANCE INDEX (SRI)

Self-reliance index (SRI) is the proportion of the share of income generated from local sources and the total revenue generated from all sources.

Based on the data provided in Table 4.3 below, the SRI of the City of Santa Rosa ranges 58% to 80% from 2018 to 2022 with an average of 71.08%. On the other hand, the average revenue sourced from IRA/NTA is approximately 22% of the total revenue from all sources and ranges from 15% to 29% for the mentioned period. This implies that City of Santa is independent of the IRA/NTA.

Figure 4.1 shows that the city had the least IRA dependency ratio among the Local Government Units in Laguna for the year 2018.

To realize this vision, the city government continues to strengthen its partnership with the private sectors and development partners and enhances its practice of participatory governance to its stakeholders.

###### STRATEGIES

The COVID 19 pandemic challenged the city in all aspects and gave window for the city to assess its resiliency. Data from the above tables show the effectivity of the strategies used during those challenging times as the city continues to approach the succeeding years with the same strategies. Below are the enhanced and improved mobilization strategies for the city’s sustainability and better resilience.

- 1. Intensify Tax Mapping. An established and complete inventory of all real properties and businesses through digitization to intensify the tax mapping in the city will consequently boost its tax collection and ease-of-doing business.
- 2. Tax Amnesty Grants. Republic Act No. 11213 known as the Tax Amnesty Act grants amnesty to all unpaid Internal Revenue taxes imposed by the National Government for Taxable Year 2017 and prior years with respect to estate tax, other internal revenue taxes, and tax on delinquencies until June 15, 2021. The tax amnesty grant was extended to June 24, 2023 as Section 6 of RA 11213 was amended and enacted through RA 11569. With this law, the number of tax delinquents will be diminished and the city’s revenue collection and administration will be enhanced.
- 3. Online Services and Transactions. Services provided that are partially online to minimize transaction time are improved and systems that were installed are upgraded as the number of offices computerized their services. E-registrations and E-payments will be fully implemented and digitization of services and transactions will be maximized through its centralization from city level down to barangay level.
- 4. All-Year Round Business One Stop Shop. Business One Stop Shop (BOSS) boosted the collection in business fees and licenses which contributed to the significant increase of the city’s local revenue collection. Thus, the city will establish the program all year round, along with the construction of the City Government’s Business Center where all regulatory offices which is part of the business permit processing will be located.
- 5. Strengthening MSMEs and Cooperatives. Seminars and workshops to capacitate MSMEs and Cooperatives will be enriched through building up their financial literacy and capability.

#### 4.4. PLAN IMPLEMENTATION, MONITORING AND EVALUATION

The City of Santa Rosa Project Monitoring Team (PMT) with the City Planning and Development Office (CPDO) as the Secretariat will be responsible on the Monitoring and Evaluation (M&E) of the Programs and Plans of the CDP 2022-2028. Implementation of the Programs and Projects identified in the CDP 2022-2028 will be monitored semiannually.

Below are recommendations provided as guide for the improvement of this plan.

- 1. An immediate review and update by the Local Finance Committee on the Projected Revenue and Expenditure to precisely adjust the investment financing potential of the LGU.
- 2. Conduct of review and assessment of projects undertaken from the 2023 Annual Investment Program to determine the financial capability to fund future requirements in implementing programs and projects.
- 3. A regular review of the Local Development Investment Plan to update and include additional programs and projects and to ensure alignment among plans.
- 4. A regular joint planning exercise by the Local Planning Committee and the Office of the City Planning and Development Coordinator in order to plan, implement, monitor and evaluate Programs, Projects and Activities from all the Local Government Unit (LGU) Departments and National Government Agencies (NGA) operating within the city for a more responsive growth and development plan for the City of Santa Rosa.

#### TERMS AND DEFINITIONS

Agricultural Lands (in forestlands) are areas that are extensively used for the production of agricultural crops. These lands are primarily intended for cultivation, livestock production and agroforestry.

Agroforestry is a suitable management farm and which increases overall production combines agricultural crops, tree crops and forest plants and/or animals simultaneously or sequentially and applies management practices which are compatible with the cultural patterns of the local populations.

Aquatic Resources includes fish, all other aquatic flora and fauna and other living resources of the aquatic environment, including, but not limited to, salt and corals.

Barangay is the smallest political unit in the country.

Building is defined as any structure built, designed or intended for the enclosure, shelter or protection of any person, animal or property. It consists of one or more rooms and/or other spaces, covered by a roof, and usually enclosed within external walls or with common dividing walls with adjacent buildings, which usually extend from the foundation to the roof.

Capacity is a combination of all strengths and resources available within a community, society or organization that can reduce the level of risk, or effects of a disaster. Capacity may include infrastructure and physical means, institutions, societal coping abilities, as well as human knowledge, skills, and collective attributes such as social relationships, leadership and management. Capacity may also be described as capability. (RA 10121)

Capacity Development is the process by which people, organizations and society systematically stimulate and develop their capacities over time to achieve social and economic goals, including through improvement of knowledge, skills, systems, and institutions.

Citizenship is defined as the legal nationality of a person.

Climate Change is the change of climate which is attributed directly or indirectly to human activity that alters the composition of the global atmosphere and which is in addition to natural climate variability observed over comparable time periods.

Collection shall refer to the act of removing solid waste from source or from a common storage point.

Commercial Fishing is the taking of fishery species by passive or active gear for trade, business or profit beyond subsistence or sports fishing.

Community-Based Disaster Risk Reduction and Management or CBDRRM is a process of disaster risk reduction and management in which at risk communities are actively engaged in the

identification, analysis, treatment, monitoring and evaluation of disaster risks in order to reduce their vulnerabilities and enhance their capacities, and where the people are at the heart of decision making and implementation of disaster risk reduction and management activities. (RA 10121)

Composting shall refer to the systematic decomposition of organic matter by micro-organisms, mainly bacteria and fungi, into a humus-like product.

Conservation shall refer to all the processes and measures of maintaining the cultural significance of a cultural property, including but not limited to, preservation, restoration, reconstruction, protection, adaptive re-use or any combination thereof.

Disability refers to any restriction or lack of ability (resulting from an impairment) to perform an activity in the manner or within the range considered normal for a human being. Impairments associated with disabilities may be physical, mental, or sensory motor impairment such as partial or total blindness, low vision, partial or total deafness, oral defect, having only one hand/no hands, one leg/no legs, mild or severe cerebral palsy, retarded, mentally ill, mental retardation and multiple impairment

Disaster is a serious disruption of the functioning of a community or a society involving widespread human, material, economic or environmental losses and impacts, which exceeds the ability of the affected community or society to cope using its own resources. (RA 10121)

Disaster Preparedness is the knowledge and capacities developed by governments, professional response and recovery organizations, communities and individuals to effectively anticipate, respond to, and recover from, the impacts of likely, imminent or current hazard events or conditions.

Disaster Prevention is the outright avoidance of adverse impacts of hazards and related disasters. It expresses the concept and intention to completely avoid potential adverse impacts through action taken in advance such as construction of dams or embankments that eliminate flood risks, land-use regulations that do not permit any settlement in high-risk areas, and seismic engineering designs that ensure the survival and function of a critical building in any likely earthquake. (RA 10121)

Disaster Response is the provision of emergency services and public assistance during or immediately after a disaster in order to save lives, reduce health impacts, ensure public safety and meet the basic subsistence needs of the people affected.

Disaster Risk is the potential disaster losses in lives, health status, livelihood, assets and services, which could occur to a particular community or a society over some specified future time period. (RA 10121)

Disaster Risk Reduction is the concept and practice of reducing disaster risks through systematic efforts to analyze and manage the causal factors of disasters, including through reduced

exposures to hazards, lessened vulnerability of people and property, wise management of land and the environment, and improved preparedness for adverse events. (RA 10121)

Disaster Risk Reduction and Management is the systematic process of using administrative directives, organizations, and operational skills and capacities to implement strategies, policies and improved coping capacities in order to lessen the adverse impacts of hazards and the possibility of disaster.

Disposal shall refer to the discharge, deposit, dumping, spilling, leaking or placing of any solid waste into or in any land.

Early Warning System is the set of capacities needed to generate and disseminate timely and meaningful warning information to enable individuals, communities and organizations threatened by a hazard to prepare and to act appropriately and in sufficient time to reduce the possibility of harm or loss. (RA 10121)

Earthquake –a sudden and violent shaking of the ground, sometimes causing great destruction, as a result of movements within the earth’s crust or volcanic action.

Earthquake-induced Landslide –a landslide hazard occurred with a delay in time and over a wider area, which triggered by post-seismic factor (earthquake).

Ecological solid waste management refers to the systematic administration of activities which provide for segregation at source, segregated transportation, storage, transfer, processing, treatment, and disposal of solid waste and all other waste management activities which do not harm the environment.

Ethnicity is a primary sense of belonging to an ethnic group. It refers to the household member’s identity, by blood and not by choice nor by adoption/confirmation for any ethnic group, primarily the Indigenous Peoples (IPs).

Ethnic group is consanguine in nature, meaning, the ties are reckoned by blood and traced

through the family tree.

Exposure is the degree to which the elements at risk are likely to experience hazard events of different magnitudes. (RA 10121)

Fish and Fishery/Aquatic Products –include not only fin fish but also mollusks, crustaceans, echinoderms, marine mammals, and all other species of aquatic flora and fauna and all other products of aquatic living resources in any form.

Fish Pond is a land-based facility enclosed with earthen or stone material to impound water for growing fish.

Fisherfolk refers to people directly or personally and physically engaged in taking and/or culturing and processing fishery and/or aquatic resources.

Fisheries refers to all activities relating to the act or business of fishing, culturing, preserving, processing, marketing, developing, conserving and managing aquatic resource.

Flood is an abnormal progressive rise in the water level of a stream that may result in the overflowing by the water of the normal confines of the stream with the subsequent inundation of areas which are not normally submerged.

Flora are all species of plants found in a given area, including ferns, lycopods and mosses. Forest Land Use refers to the manner of utilization of forestlands, including their allocation, development and management. Forest Reservation/ Reserves refer to forestlands which have been reserved by the President of the Philippines for any specific purpose or purposes. (PD 705)

Geographic Information System (GIS) is a database which contains, among others, geo-hazard assessments, information on climate change, and climate risk reduction and management (RA 10121). It is defined as an information system that is used to input, store, retrieve, manipulate, analyze and output geographically referenced data or geospatial data.

Ground shaking is the primary cause of earthquake damage to man-made structures.

Hazard is a dangerous phenomenon, substance human activity or condition that may cause loss of life, injury or other health impacts, property damage, loss of livelihood and services, social and economic disruption, or environmental damage. (RA 10121)

Hazardous waste refers to solid waste or combination of solid waste which because of its quantity, concentration, or physical, chemical or infectious characteristics may; cause, or significantly contribute to an increase in mortality or an increase in serious irreversible, or incapacitating reversible illness; or pose a substantial present or potential hazard to human health or the environment when improperly treated, stored, transported, or disposed of, or otherwise managed.

History shall refer to a written record of past events relating to Philippine history

Household is a social unit consisting of a person living alone or a group of person who sleep in the same housing unit and have a common arrangement in the preparation and consumption of food.

Housing unit is a structurally separate and independent place of abode which, by the way it has been constructed, converted, or arranged, is intended for habitation by one or more households.

Institutional Living Quarter (ILQ) is the place of abode of an institutional population. It is a structurally separate and independent place of abode intended for habitation by large groups of individuals (10 or more). Such quarter usually has certain common facilities such as kitchen and dining rooms, toilet and bath, and lounging areas, which are shared by the occupants.

Institutional population comprises of persons who are found living in ILQs. They may have their own families or households elsewhere but at the time of census, they are committed or confined in institutions, or they live in ILQs and are usually subject to a common authority or management, or are bound by either a common public objective or a common personal interest.

Large scale commercial fishing is fishing utilizing active gears and vessels of more than one hundred fifty (150) GT.

"Materials recovery facility" shall include solid waste transfer station or sorting station, drop-off center, a composting facility, and a recycling facility.

Medium scale commercial fishing is fishing utilizing active gears and vessels of 20.1 GT up to one hundred fifty (150) GT.

Mitigation measures encompass engineering techniques and hazard resistant construction as well as improved environmental policies and public awareness. (RA 10121)

Non-governmental organization (NGO) is an agency, institution, a foundation or a group of persons whose purpose is to assist people’s organizations/associations in various ways including, but not limited to, organizing, education, training, and research.

Preparedness is the knowledge and capacities developed by governments, professional response and recovery organizations, communities and individuals to effectively anticipate, respond to, and recover from, the impacts of likely, imminent or current hazard events or conditions. Prevention is the outright avoidance of adverse impacts of hazards and related disasters.

Prospective disaster risk reduction and management refers to risk reduction and management activities that address and seek to avoid the development of new or increased disaster risks, especially if risk reduction policies are not put in place. (RA 10121)

Protected Areas are identified portions of land and water set aside by reason of their unique physical and biological significance and are managed to enhance biological diversity and protected against destructive human exploitation as provided for in RA 7586, otherwise known as the National Integrated Protected Areas Systems (NIPAS) ACT of 1992.

Rainfall-Induced Landslide is a landslide hazard occurred with a delay in time and over a wider area, which triggered by post-seismic factor (rainfall)

Rehabilitation measures that ensure the ability of affected communities/areas to restore their normal level of functioning by rebuilding livelihood and damaged infrastructures and increasing the communities’ organizational capacity. (RA 10121)

Religious affiliation refers to a particular system of beliefs, attitudes, emotions, and behaviors constituting man’s relationship with the powers and principalities of the universe.

Resilience is the ability of a system, community or society exposed to hazards to resist, absorb, accommodate and recover from the effects of a hazard in a timely and efficient manner, including through the preservation and restoration of its essential basic structures and functions. (RA 10121)

Response is any concerted effort by two (2) or more agencies, public or private, to provide assistance or intervention during or immediately after a disaster to meet the life preservation and basic subsistence needs of those people affected and in the restoration of essential public activities and facilities. (RA 10121)

Restoration shall refer to the action taken or the technical intervention to correct deterioration and alterations.

Risk is the combination of the probability of an event and its negative consequences.

Risk Assessment is a methodology to determine the nature and extent of risk by analyzing potential hazards and evaluating existing conditions of vulnerability that together could potentially harm exposed people, property, services, livelihood and the environment on which they depend.

Risk Management is the systematic approach and practice of managing uncertainty to minimize potential harm and loss.

Segregation refers to sorting and segregation of different materials found in solid waste in order to promote recycling and re-use of resources and to reduce the volume of waste for collection and disposal.

Small scale commercial fishing is fishing with passive or active gear utilizing fishing vessels of 3.1 gross tons (GT) up to twenty (20) GT.

Solid waste refers to all discarded household, commercial waste, non-hazardous institutional, ports / harbour and industrial waste, street sweepings, construction debris, agriculture waste, and other non-hazardous/non-toxic solid waste.

Solid waste management is the discipline associated with the control of generation, storage, collection, transfer and transport, processing, and disposal of solid wastes in a manner that is in accord with the best principles of public health, economics, engineering, conservation,

aesthetics, and other environmental considerations, and that is also responsive to public attitudes.

Solid waste management facility is any resource recovery system or component thereof; any system, program, or facility for resource conservation; any facility for the collection, source separation, storage, transportation, transfer, processing, treatment, or disposal of solid waste.

Special wastes are household hazardous wastes such as paints, thinners, household batteries, lead-acid batteries, spray canisters and the like. These include wastes from residential and commercial sources that comprise of bulky wastes, consumer electronics, white goods, yard wastes that are collected separately, batteries, oil, and tires. These wastes are usually handled separately from other residential and commercial wastes.

Storage is the interim containment of solid waste after generation and prior to collection for ultimate recovery or disposal.

Susceptibility means open or subject to hazards. Vulnerability is the characteristics and circumstances of a community, system or asset that make it susceptible to the damaging effects of a hazard.
